Algebra 1 — Unit 5: Factoring and Quadratic Functions — Canonical Bank

Teacher-facing canonical content factory. Secure items and solutions are included for authorized downstream assembly.

What's To Come — 5.1

U5-S1-WTC01

Factor the greatest common factor from $48x^2+18x$.
Before formal instruction, record one feature of the displayed representation that could help you test your conclusion.

Teacher answer / solution

$6x(8x^1+3)$. A useful feature should be tied to the displayed graph or structure.

Both terms share $6x$. Dividing each term by \$6x$ leaves \$8x^1+3$.

Notes Example — 5.1

U5-S1-NOTE-EX01

Factor the greatest common factor from $30x^2+12x$.

Teacher answer / solution

$3x(10x^1+4)$.

Both terms share $3x$. Dividing each term by \$3x$ leaves \$10x^1+4$.

Notes You Try It — 5.1

U5-S1-NOTE-YTI01

Factor the greatest common factor from $36x^2+36x$.

Teacher answer / solution

$12x(3x^1+3)$.

Both terms share $12x$. Dividing each term by \$12x$ leaves \$3x^1+3$.

Notes Example — 5.1

U5-S1-NOTE-EX02

Teacher answer / solution

$3x(3x^1+11)$.

Both terms share $3x$. Dividing each term by \$3x$ leaves \$3x^1+11$.

Notes You Try It — 5.1

Teacher answer / solution

Both terms share $2x$. Dividing each term by \$2x$ leaves \$9x^2+6$.

What's To Come — 5.2

U5-S2-WTC01

Factor $x^2-8x-48$ completely.
Before formal instruction, record one feature of the displayed representation that could help you test your conclusion.

Teacher answer / solution

$(x+4)(x-12)$. A useful feature should be tied to the displayed graph or structure.

Find numbers with product -48 and sum -8: 4 and -12. Therefore $x^2-8x-48=(x+4)(x-12)$.

Notes Example — 5.2

U5-S2-NOTE-EX01

Factor $x^2+5x+6$ completely.

Teacher answer / solution

$(x+2)(x+3)$.

Find numbers with product 6 and sum 5: 2 and 3. Therefore $x^2+5x+6=(x+2)(x+3)$.

Notes You Try It — 5.2

U5-S2-NOTE-YTI01

Factor $x^2+12x-13$ completely.

Teacher answer / solution

$(x-1)(x+13)$.

Find numbers with product -13 and sum 12: -1 and 13. Therefore $x^2+12x-13=(x-1)(x+13)$.

Notes Example — 5.2

U5-S2-NOTE-EX02

Factor $x^2+22x+120$ completely.

Teacher answer / solution

$(x+12)(x+10)$.

Find numbers with product 120 and sum 22: 12 and 10. Therefore $x^2+22x+120=(x+12)(x+10)$.

Notes You Try It — 5.2

U5-S2-NOTE-YTI02

Teacher answer / solution

$(x+3)(x+3)$.

Find numbers with product 9 and sum 6: 3 and 3. Therefore $x^2+6x+9=(x+3)(x+3)$.

What's To Come — 5.3

U5-S3-WTC01

Factor $64x^2-36$.
Before formal instruction, record one feature of the displayed representation that could help you test your conclusion.

Teacher answer / solution

$(8x-6)(8x+6)$. A useful feature should be tied to the displayed graph or structure.

This is a difference of squares: $A^2-B^2=(A-B)(A+B)$.

Notes Example — 5.3

Teacher answer / solution

$(3x-12)(3x+12)$.

This is a difference of squares: $A^2-B^2=(A-B)(A+B)$.

What's To Come — 5.4

U5-S4-WTC01

For $y=(x+10)(x-8)$, state the zeros and the x-intercepts.
Before formal instruction, record one feature of the displayed representation that could help you test your conclusion.

Teacher answer / solution

Zeros: $x=-10$ and $x=8$; x-intercepts: $(-10,0)$ and $(8,0)$. A useful feature should be tied to the displayed graph or structure.

Set each factor equal to zero. Each zero becomes an x-intercept on the graph.

Notes Example — 5.4

U5-S4-NOTE-EX01

For $y=(x+5)(x-7)$, state the zeros and the x-intercepts.

Teacher answer / solution

Zeros: $x=-5$ and $x=7$; x-intercepts: $(-5,0)$ and $(7,0)$.

Set each factor equal to zero. Each zero becomes an x-intercept on the graph.

Notes You Try It — 5.4

Set each factor equal to zero. Each zero becomes an x-intercept on the graph.

What's To Come — 5.5

U5-S5-WTC01

Which form would reveal the vertex most directly for a quadratic with vertex $(0,-10)$: standard, factored, or vertex form? Write a matching vertex-form equation with leading coefficient 1.
Before formal instruction, record one feature of the displayed representation that could help you test your conclusion.

Teacher answer / solution

Vertex form; $y=(x+0)^2-10$. A useful feature should be tied to the displayed graph or structure.

Vertex form $y=a(x-h)^2+k$ displays the vertex $(h,k)$ directly.

Notes Example — 5.5

U5-S5-NOTE-EX01

Which form would reveal the vertex most directly for a quadratic with vertex $(-8,0)$: standard, factored, or vertex form? Write a matching vertex-form equation with leading coefficient 1.

Teacher answer / solution

Vertex form; $y=(x+8)^2+0$.

Vertex form $y=a(x-h)^2+k$ displays the vertex $(h,k)$ directly.

Notes You Try It — 5.5

U5-S5-NOTE-YTI01

Vertex form $y=a(x-h)^2+k$ displays the vertex $(h,k)$ directly.

U5-S1-P2-M01

Solve the system $y=5x-56$ and $y=-2x+21$. Give the ordered-pair solution and verify it in both equations.

Teacher answer / solution

$(11,-1)$.

Set the expressions for $y$ equal or eliminate a variable. The equations meet at $x=11$ and $y=-1$, so the solution is $(11,-1)$. Substitution satisfies both equations.

U5-S1-P2-I06

Factor the greatest common factor from $21x^2+77x$.

Teacher answer / solution

$7x(3x^1+11)$.

Both terms share $7x$. Dividing each term by \$7x$ leaves \$3x^1+11$.

Practice Set 1 — 5.2

U5-S2-P1-I01

Factor $x^2+13x+22$ completely.

Teacher answer / solution

$(x+2)(x+11)$.

Find numbers with product 22 and sum 13: 2 and 11. Therefore $x^2+13x+22=(x+2)(x+11)$.

U5-S2-P1-I02

Factor $x^2+17x+72$ completely.

Teacher answer / solution

$(x+9)(x+8)$.

Find numbers with product 72 and sum 17: 9 and 8. Therefore $x^2+17x+72=(x+9)(x+8)$.

U5-S2-P1-I03

Factor $x^2+6x-55$ completely.

Teacher answer / solution

$(x+11)(x-5)$.

Find numbers with product -55 and sum 6: 11 and -5. Therefore $x^2+6x-55=(x+11)(x-5)$.

U5-S2-P1-I04

Factor $x^2+5x-104$ completely.

Teacher answer / solution

$(x-8)(x+13)$.

Find numbers with product -104 and sum 5: -8 and 13. Therefore $x^2+5x-104=(x-8)(x+13)$.

U5-S2-P1-I05

Factor $x^2-121$ completely.

Teacher answer / solution

$(x-11)(x+11)$.

Find numbers with product -121 and sum 0: -11 and 11. Therefore $x^2-121=(x-11)(x+11)$.

U5-S2-P1-I06

Factor $x^2+x-56$ completely.

Teacher answer / solution

$(x-7)(x+8)$.

Find numbers with product -56 and sum 1: -7 and 8. Therefore $x^2+x-56=(x-7)(x+8)$.

U5-S2-P1-I07

Factor $x^2+13x+36$ completely.

Teacher answer / solution

$(x+4)(x+9)$.

Find numbers with product 36 and sum 13: 4 and 9. Therefore $x^2+13x+36=(x+4)(x+9)$.

U5-S2-P1-I08

Factor $x^2+x-30$ completely.

Teacher answer / solution

$(x+6)(x-5)$.

Find numbers with product -30 and sum 1: 6 and -5. Therefore $x^2+x-30=(x+6)(x-5)$.

U5-S2-P1-R01

The quadratic $y=2x^2-18x-180$ has zeros $x=-6$ and $x=15$. Explain how those zeros connect to a factored form and the graph.

Teacher answer / solution

A matching factored form is $y=2(x+6)(x-15)$; the graph crosses or touches the x-axis at $(-6,0)$ and $(15,0)$.

A matching factored form is $y=2(x+6)(x-15)$; the graph crosses or touches the x-axis at $(-6,0)$ and $(15,0)$.

U5-S2-P1-R02

The quadratic $y=x^2-5x-126$ has zeros $x=-9$ and $x=14$. Explain how those zeros connect to a factored form and the graph.

Teacher answer / solution

A matching factored form is $y=(x+9)(x-14)$; the graph crosses or touches the x-axis at $(-9,0)$ and $(14,0)$.

A matching factored form is $y=(x+9)(x-14)$; the graph crosses or touches the x-axis at $(-9,0)$ and $(14,0)$.

U5-S2-P1-R03

The quadratic $y=2x^2+8x-154$ has zeros $x=-11$ and $x=7$. Explain how those zeros connect to a factored form and the graph.

Teacher answer / solution

A matching factored form is $y=2(x+11)(x-7)$; the graph crosses or touches the x-axis at $(-11,0)$ and $(7,0)$.

Practice Set 1 — 5.3

U5-S3-P1-I01

Factor $36x^2-100$.

Teacher answer / solution

$(6x-10)(6x+10)$.

This is a difference of squares: $A^2-B^2=(A-B)(A+B)$.

U5-S3-P1-I02

Factor $x^2+6x+9$.

Teacher answer / solution

$(x+3)^2$.

The first and last terms are squares and the middle term is $2(3)x$, so the expression is $(x+3)^2$.

U5-S4-P1-I01

For $y=(x+8)(x-5)$, state the zeros and the x-intercepts.

Teacher answer / solution

Zeros: $x=-8$ and $x=5$; x-intercepts: $(-8,0)$ and $(5,0)$.

Set each factor equal to zero. Each zero becomes an x-intercept on the graph.

U5-S4-P1-R01

Start with $y=|x|$. Describe the transformations that produce $y=-2|x-7|+1$, including the effect of the outside factor.

Teacher answer / solution

Shift horizontally by 7 unit(s) right, shift 1 unit(s) up, and apply an outside factor of -2.

Shift horizontally by 7 unit(s) right, shift 1 unit(s) up, and apply an outside factor of -2. The negative factor reflects the graph across the x-axis.

U5-S4-P1-R02

Start with $y=|x|$. Describe the transformations that produce $y=-2|x-2|-9$, including the effect of the outside factor.

Teacher answer / solution

Shift horizontally by 2 unit(s) right, shift 9 unit(s) down, and apply an outside factor of -2.

Shift horizontally by 2 unit(s) right, shift 9 unit(s) down, and apply an outside factor of -2. The negative factor reflects the graph across the x-axis.

U5-S4-P1-R03

Start with $y=|x|$. Describe the transformations that produce $y=3|x+1|-12$, including the effect of the outside factor.

Teacher answer / solution

Shift horizontally by 1 unit(s) left, shift 12 unit(s) down, and apply an outside factor of 3.

Shift horizontally by 1 unit(s) left, shift 12 unit(s) down, and apply an outside factor of 3. The magnitude of the factor changes vertical scale.

U5-S4-P1-R04

Start with $y=2^x$. Describe the transformations that produce $y=4(2^(x+7))-5$, including the effect of the outside factor.

Teacher answer / solution

Shift horizontally by 7 unit(s) left, shift 5 unit(s) down, and apply an outside factor of 4.

Shift horizontally by 7 unit(s) left, shift 5 unit(s) down, and apply an outside factor of 4. The magnitude of the factor changes vertical scale.

U5-S4-P1-R05

Teacher answer / solution

Shift horizontally by 9 unit(s) left, shift 1 unit(s) down, and apply an outside factor of 3.

Practice Set 1 — 5.5

U5-S5-P1-I01

Which form would reveal the vertex most directly for a quadratic with vertex $(-8,3)$: standard, factored, or vertex form? Write a matching vertex-form equation with leading coefficient 1.

Teacher answer / solution

Vertex form; $y=(x+8)^2+3$.

Vertex form $y=a(x-h)^2+k$ displays the vertex $(h,k)$ directly.

Vertex form $y=a(x-h)^2+k$ displays the vertex $(h,k)$ directly.

U5-S5-PX-I06

Which form would reveal the vertex most directly for a quadratic with vertex $(-6,9)$: standard, factored, or vertex form? Write a matching vertex-form equation with leading coefficient 1.

Teacher answer / solution

Vertex form; $y=(x+6)^2+9$.

Vertex form $y=a(x-h)^2+k$ displays the vertex $(h,k)$ directly.

U5-S5-PX-I07

Which form would reveal the vertex most directly for a quadratic with vertex $(-5,7)$: standard, factored, or vertex form? Write a matching vertex-form equation with leading coefficient 1.

Teacher answer / solution

Vertex form; $y=(x+5)^2+7$.

Vertex form $y=a(x-h)^2+k$ displays the vertex $(h,k)$ directly.

U5-S5-PX-I08

Which form would reveal the vertex most directly for a quadratic with vertex $(-6,-9)$: standard, factored, or vertex form? Write a matching vertex-form equation with leading coefficient 1.

Teacher answer / solution

Vertex form; $y=(x+6)^2-9$.

Vertex form $y=a(x-h)^2+k$ displays the vertex $(h,k)$ directly.

U5-S5-PX-R05

Start with $y=|x|$. Describe the transformations that produce $y=-2|x-3|-5$, including the effect of the outside factor.

Teacher answer / solution

Shift horizontally by 3 unit(s) right, shift 5 unit(s) down, and apply an outside factor of -2.

Shift horizontally by 3 unit(s) right, shift 5 unit(s) down, and apply an outside factor of -2. The negative factor reflects the graph across the x-axis.

U5-S1-CYU-I01

Factor the greatest common factor from $18x^2+8x$.

Teacher answer / solution

$2x(9x^1+4)$.

Both terms share $2x$. Dividing each term by \$2x$ leaves \$9x^1+4$.

U5-S1-CYU-I02

Teacher answer / solution

$2x(5x^1+2)$.

Both terms share $2x$. Dividing each term by \$2x$ leaves \$5x^1+2$.

$2x(11x^1+11)$.

Both terms share $2x$. Dividing each term by \$2x$ leaves \$11x^1+11$.

$10x(4x^1+8)$.

$(x+1)(x-7)$.

Find numbers with product -7 and sum -6: 1 and -7. Therefore $x^2-6x-7=(x+1)(x-7)$.

  4. Factored form cannot show intercepts

  1. $(5x-15)(5x+15)$.
  2. $(5x-15)^2$
  3. $(5x+15)^2$
  4. $(5x-15)(5x-15)$
Teacher answer / solution

$(5x-15)(5x+15)$.

This is a difference of squares: $A^2-B^2=(A-B)(A+B)$.

U5-S3-BLK-R02

Which form would reveal the vertex most directly for a quadratic with vertex $(8,2)$: standard, factored, or vertex form? Write a matching vertex-form equation with leading coefficient 1.

  1. Vertex form; $y=(x-8)^2+2$.
  2. standard form
  3. factored form
  4. all forms reveal the vertex equally directly
Teacher answer / solution

Vertex form; $y=(x-8)^2+2$.

Vertex form $y=a(x-h)^2+k$ displays the vertex $(h,k)$ directly.

U5-S5-BLK-M01

Consider $x+y\le 39$, $x\ge 10$, and $y\ge 2$. Is $(29,10)$ feasible? Explain which constraints are satisfied.

  1. No; it violates the total constraint
  2. No; it violates the x constraint
  3. Feasible; $x+y=39$ with lower bounds $x\ge 10$ and $y\ge 2$.
  4. No; it violates the y constraint
Teacher answer / solution

Feasible; $x+y=39$ with lower bounds $x\ge 10$ and $y\ge 2$.

$x+y=39$, $x=29$, and $y=10$. Compare these with 39, 10, and 2; all required inequalities are true.

  1. No; it violates the total constraint
  2. No; it violates the x constraint
  3. Feasible; $x+y=31$ with lower bounds $x\ge 12$ and $y\ge 12$.
  4. No; it violates the y constraint
Teacher answer / solution

Feasible; $x+y=31$ with lower bounds $x\ge 12$ and $y\ge 12$.

$x+y=31$, $x=16$, and $y=15$. Compare these with 31, 12, and 12; all required inequalities are true.

  1. No; it violates the total constraint
  2. Feasible; $x+y=21$ with lower bounds $x\ge 8$ and $y\ge 7$.

  4. No; it violates the y constraint

U5-S1-WU1-PK03

Solve the system $y=3x-15$ and $y=-6x+66$. Give the ordered-pair solution and verify it in both equations.

Teacher answer / solution

$(9,12)$.

Set the expressions for $y$ equal or eliminate a variable. The equations meet at $x=9$ and $y=12$, so the solution is $(9,12)$. Substitution satisfies both equations.

Warm-Up 2 — 5.1

U5-S1-WU2-I01

Factor the greatest common factor from $50x^3+35x$.

Teacher answer / solution

$5x(10x^2+7)$.

Both terms share $5x$. Dividing each term by \$5x$ leaves \$10x^2+7$.
